# Capability status — one compact row per capability

The baseline recorded here was the repository at protected develop `2c573a24`, measured 2026-09-16.

**Re-derived 2026-09-18 against protected develop `5b2c7840`**, after the six directive pull requests
merged (#410 gates, #411 environment, #412 search, #413 organisation administration, #414 platform
console, #415 inventory, #416 alerts). Every row below was checked against that head: its status, its
actual UI route, the backend operation the screen calls, and what the evidence for it is.

**Section 2 re-read 2026-09-19 on `feature/owner-directive-console-account-security`**, at its merge
with develop `1a90f356`. That branch adds the operator's own account and security page and closes
four gaps a completeness pass found in the console; the Platform Owner Console rows below say what
the console does on that branch rather than what it did before it. The re-reading is the same kind of
evidence as the rest of this file — the route file, the screen and the operation were opened and
read.

**Both owed proofs were then taken, between 2026-09-19 and 2026-09-21, and most of this file is no
longer owed anything.** A measurement campaign worked the product by hand on the local acceptance
stack — in a real browser and over the service — against three heads of `develop` in turn
(`1a90f356`, `b1814119` and finally `f30ce918`), using two controlled organisations created for the
purpose. Sixty of the seventy-one rows that carried an owed-proof marker now carry instead
**`measured <date> on the local acceptance stack`**, naming the day that row's behaviour was read
back from the product. Twelve rows still carry the owed marker — the eleven that were never driven,
plus the new row for adding a second platform operator — and they are listed at the end of this
file.

**What a `measured` marker claims, and what it does not.** It claims that the capability was
exercised on the local environment on that date and that the product's own answer was read back —
including where that answer was a refusal, in which case the row says so. It is **not** a claim that
any check, certification, audit or approval passed, and it is not a claim about any environment
other than the local one, because no other environment exists. Where the campaign measured a
capability and found a gap, the row states the gap and names the change-control row that carries it.

## The four states

- **implemented** — a user can reach the capability through a route in the web application, the
  route calls a backend operation that exists, and the evidence column names where that was
  demonstrated. Where an operation exists and works but has no screen, the row says
  `implemented (API only)` and is not a completed workflow.
- **partial** — some of the capability exists and a stated part of it does not. The gap is written
  in the row, not left to be inferred.
- **missing** — no route, no operation, or neither.
- **externally blocked** — the capability cannot be completed inside this repository, and the
  reason names what is outside it.

A row needs all three of a UI route, a backend operation and evidence before it reads
**implemented**. A plan is not evidence. An API without its screen is not a completed workflow. A
screenshot of a screen that performed no transaction is not evidence that the transaction works.

## What the evidence column says at this revision, and what it does not

**Read this before reading any row.** The re-derivation of 2026-09-18 was a **code reading**: for
each row, the route file, the screen, the adapter and the backend operation were opened and read at
`5b2c7840`. That is what the evidence column reports, and it is enough to tell you that a screen
exists, which operation it calls, and what it refuses.

**Two proofs were owed for the work these six pull requests merged, and both have now been taken —
though neither completely.** They were:

| Owed proof                              | What it would establish                                                                                                            | Where it stands                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Authenticated console browser walk**  | That a signed-in platform owner can open every console screen and complete a transaction on it.                                    | Taken 2026-09-19 and 2026-09-20, for every console row except suspend, reactivate and close.           |
| **Acceptance journey for the new work** | That the inventory, organisation-administration, search and alert capabilities run end to end over the service against a database. | Taken between 2026-09-19 and 2026-09-21, for most rows; six administration rows and two others remain. |

A row that still carries **`proof owed`** names what is owed. It is **not** a claim that the
capability is broken; it is a claim that the code implementing it was read at this head and the
behaviour was not exercised.

**The count, so that it can be checked against the tables:** the seven capability sections of this file hold **99 capability rows**
— one more than at the previous revision, because the technician roster and profile now exist
(`P1-32-PRE-OD-ADM2B`); the revision before added one because the item page now reads its own item and the
item update it still lacks is a gap of its own (`P1-32-PRE-OD-INV2A`); the revision before added
a second platform operator as a capability with a supported procedure. **Sixty carry a `measured`
marker with its date; twelve still carry the owed-proof marker; the remaining 27 carry no marker**, for the reasons
in the next paragraph. The twelve are listed at the end of this file. The sections added at the end of this file for the Owner directive of 2026-10-01 (the delivery matrix with its checkpoint and feature tables, and the accounting section) are not capability rows: their rows are counted separately in those sections and change none of the figures in this paragraph.

**A row with no marker is not a demonstrated row either.** It means one of three things, and the row
itself says which: the capability is `missing` or `externally blocked`, so there is nothing to
demonstrate; the row records a document rather than a running capability; or the capability predates
these six pull requests and is unchanged by them, in which case whatever evidence exists for it
belongs to the earlier phase that delivered it and was not re-taken here. No hosted run, acceptance
run, certification or approval is claimed anywhere in this file.

## The twelve rows that still carry an owed-proof marker

Listed once, so the remainder is a short list rather than something to be searched for:

| Section | Row                                   | What is owed                                                                                           |
| ------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 2       | Suspend, reactivate and close         | The three lifecycle actions were not driven; every other console row was.                              |
| 2       | Platform operator identity            | None of the three provisioning scripts was run by any measurement.                                     |
| 1/11    | Adding a second platform operator     | The same: the addition script was read, not run.                                                       |
| 3       | Create a company                      | Creating one from inside an organisation was not driven; the console side of it was.                   |
| 3       | Create a branch                       | The same.                                                                                              |
| 3       | Take a role away                      | Granting and scoping were driven; revoking was not.                                                    |
| 3       | Read one person's roles and sessions  | The detail screen was not opened.                                                                      |
| 3       | Revoke sessions                       | Not driven.                                                                                            |
| 3       | Company status                        | Not driven.                                                                                            |
| 4       | Idempotency on writes                 | No repeat of a write was made deliberately to observe the stated replayed outcome.                     |
| 7       | Work order by number                  | Searching a work order by its number was not driven.                                                   |
| 1/11    | Startup validation of required values | A start-up with `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_ENV` set to `staging` or `production`, which no environment performs. |

Counted plainly, and checkable against the tables above: **twelve rows carry an owed marker, sixty
carry a measured marker, twenty-five carry no marker, and the file holds 97 capability rows.**

## What the measurement campaign found that is not a row above

Four things it measured are gaps rather than capabilities, so they are change-control rows rather
than status rows. They are named here because a reader of this file will look for them:

- **The plan named while provisioning an organisation does not take effect** until a subscription is
  assigned to that organisation afterwards. CC-OD-24.
- **Nobody in a provisioned organisation can record a unit cost.** CC-OD-25, cross-referring to
  CC-12 of the P1-30 register, which files the exclusion as deliberate.
- **Nobody in a provisioned organisation can approve a credit note.** CC-OD-26.
- **A provisioned organisation has no vehicle makes or models**, which also makes a vehicle service
  capacity impossible to record. CC-OD-28.

Two further rows record smaller findings: the material-request refusal that did not say which of
its two causes applied (CC-OD-27, closed by PR #430, with CC-OD-32 recording what that fix did not
cover), and the absence of any revocation path for platform authority (CC-OD-29, decided and implemented
2026-09-22 by `scripts/platform/revoke-platform-operator.mjs` — the row above records it, and the
provider-side session residual W9-R1 remains). A drill-through that fills a report in without
running it is CC-OD-30.

## Owner directive of 2026-10-01: delivery matrix

**Revisions measured.**

- Running acceptance build: `d5ce97b7` (rebuilt 2026-10-08 after the CP-20261008-2 database step; its database ledger holds 186 migrations, the same 186 versions as the files at `d5ce97b7`, compared read-only on 2026-10-08; outside repository, `orchestration/evidence/owner-directive-cp-20261008-2-d2-d17-runtime/DB-APPLY.md`). Before 2026-10-08 this line read: `f130fc06` (built 2026-10-01; its database ledger held 165 migrations, per the coordinator's record).
- Develop: `d5ce97b7` (the merge of records pull request #538, 2026-10-08), the same revision as the running build. Before 2026-10-08 this line read: `ec91239b`, adding #493 (CI time limit), #494 (finance fixes A) and #495 (finance fixes B), none of the three then on the running build.
- Corrected 2026-10-04 at develop `02a0462c` (P1-32-PRE-OD-LIC): D4 and D14 delivered; fix PR E and the D12 extension added; the checkpoints at `bfe4e773`, `c8940b1c` and `5d3dcbec` added; ADR-023 line numbers in this section and the next re-anchored by content to `02a0462c`. The rest of the matrix is as measured at `ec91239b`.
- Refreshed 2026-10-08 at develop `d5ce97b7` (P1-32-PRE-OD-WP00, importing the completion plan of 2026-10-08): the two revisions above, the migration count, the Sales and finance, Reporting, Printing and Release rows, the count sentence, the checkpoint row `d5ce97b7`, dated evidence in the verification ledger, and the ADR-023 mapping-table pointers (the table now stands at `ADR-023-sales-and-finance-policy-decisions.md:751-773`). Pull requests #513 to #538 are the evidence. Every other row is as measured before.

**Migrations.** Develop `d5ce97b7` holds 186 `.sql` files under `supabase/migrations` (listed from git on 2026-10-08; the derived marker `deliverable-manifest.md:991` reads 186 as well). The newest is `20261008140000_sal_refund_requests.sql`. The acceptance database's ledger holds the same 186 versions (read-only comparison, 2026-10-08), so no migration is pending at develop. A merged migration cannot be changed or removed (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:319-332`). Before 2026-10-08 this paragraph read: develop held 165 files (counted 2026-10-02), the newest `20261001090000_sal_credit_approval_limits.sql`, and the equal count **indicated** that #493 to #495 added no migration, an inference from counts, not from a diff, with the running build's count coordinator-reported.

**What "verified" means here.** A signed-in browser checkpoint, or a dated `measured` marker in this file's earlier sections, passed at the revision and date named. Merged code is never counted as verified. Navigation filtered by permission is never counted as a modular commercial system. Measurements taken on the interface of their time are not re-measurements of the new interface. Browser checkpoints are cited by revision and date only.

**How the five columns map onto this file's four states** (D-OD-10 in `README.md`). _Completed and verified_ is implemented with a dated marker or checkpoint. _Implemented, awaiting integration or verification_ is implemented or partial without one. _Remaining in current scope_ is missing or partial. _Blocked on accounting answers_ is externally blocked, and names the accounting questionnaire. _Future expansion_ is outside current scope.

**This section owns verification state only.** The implementation state of D1 to D17 is in ADR-023's mapping table (`ADR-023-sales-and-finance-policy-decisions.md:751-773` at develop `d5ce97b7`, re-anchored 2026-10-08; it stood at `:703-721` before later additions to ADR-023 moved it). What can proceed and what is blocked is in the next section. The couplings and focused changes cited here (C-01 to C-17, FC-01 to FC-20) are stated in `docs/platform/architecture-assessment-2026-10-01.md`, and their dispositions are rows in `change-control.md`.

**The count, kept apart from the 97 capability rows:** the matrix below holds 17 area rows, the checkpoint table 19 rows and the feature table 45 rows. The TDP-2026-10 hosted checkpoint register below holds 9 rows and the verification ledger 32 rows (recounted from the tables on 2026-10-08; until then this sentence read 16, 43, 6 and 29; 31 before VL-P132-015 was added by P1-32-PRE-OD-FRX; the register 7 before CP-20261008-3 was added by P1-32-PRE-OD-CP-08; the checkpoint table 18 before row `5f506e0f` and the register 8 before CP-20261009-1 were added by P1-32-PRE-OD-CP-09). **CI and release notes (not product verification).**

- Develop CI is green at `ec91239b`, as reported by the coordinator.
- The clean-room limit was raised from 60 to 90 minutes (#493, Owner-authorised).
- The web-quality job limit is raised from 30 to 45 minutes (Owner-authorised 2026-10-03).
- Web DOM test speed (`feature/owner-directive-web-dom-test-speed`, Owner-authorised 2026-10-03,
  no assertion, case, coverage setting, timeout or retry changed). Cause, measured: every mount of
  the Material foundation builds a new Emotion cache, which writes each rule as its own `<style>`
  tag, and unmounting left those tags in `<head>`; jsdom's insert cost grows with `<head>`. The DOM
  setup now removes, after each case's unmount, only the styles an unmounted foundation tree could
  own (cache keys `mui` and `muirtl`, and the cascade-layer order statement). `payments.dom.test.tsx`
  is split by its top-level groups into `payments-branch-and-list`, `payments-recording`,
  `payments-allocation` and `payments-print-and-reversal` (`.dom.test.tsx`), with the shared
  fixtures in `tests/support/payments-screen.tsx`; the sorted list of 113 full test names is
  identical before and after. Local, one worker, the payments cases alone, before → after: summed
  test time 564.9 s → 128.1 s; wall 582 s → 201 s; last-quarter to first-quarter mean per case
  3.67 → 1.07; `<head>` at the last case 17,797 children (17,679 Emotion sheets) → 1 (0). Hosted,
  PR #506 head `19b3beb6`, PR CI run 37188602832, web-quality job 111395880479 (runner region
  westcentralus): job 17 min 30 s, web component tier step 10 min 40 s, summed test time 1516 s,
  the four payments files 130.4 s, 7340 tests in 193 files. For comparison, develop `5d3dcbec`
  (run 37180153256, job 111370958978, centralus): job 28 min 0 s, step 20 min 50 s, summed 3369 s,
  payments 1091.7 s; the `c8940b1c` baseline: payments 941 s, web tier mean 1106 s, job mean
  1492 s. Runner speed varies by up to 1.56 times between runs, so one run per side is an
  indication, not a mean. Coverage totals are identical to develop `5d3dcbec`'s hosted artifact
  (lines 92.56 %, statements 89.86 %, functions 92.89 %, branches 83.96 %). The three M-09
  lost-answer cases took 3.4–3.9 s on that run (3.4–3.6 s locally), so their own 60 s budget is
  removed and they run under the default 30 s one; no timeout was raised anywhere. At the next head,
  `85b7a839` (PR CI run 37189940805, web-quality job 111399906756, centralus, the same region as
  the develop run above): job 11 min 53 s, step 6 min 45 s, summed test time 967 s, the payments
  files 88.0 s, the M-09 cases 2.4–2.7 s under the default budget, coverage totals unchanged; the
  P1-27 web run record is taken from that run (7340 tests, 193 files).
- The coordinator reports that the four finance migrations (`20260930090000`, `20260930100000`, `20260930110000`, `20261001090000`) were backed up, rehearsed on a restored copy and applied forward on 2026-10-01. ADR-023 requires that step (`:384-394`); its execution has no record in the repository, so it is not counted as verified.

### Hosted checkpoint register

TDP-2026-10, the temporary development-path CI policy (Owner approval 2026-10-05; policy text, review point and restoration order in `docs/engineering/ci-automation/pr-gate.md`). One row per checkpoint, append-only: a row is never edited except to fill a blank cell, and `tests/ci/verification-ledger.test.ts` compares this table with the copy on the pull request's base. A checkpoint is the protected verification workflow dispatched on `develop` with a 40-character candidate D and an id `CP-YYYYMMDD-N`, then one records pull request merged as D'. It counts as passed only when the protected gate decided Go at D, the records pull request's STRICT gate was green with the unit and web tiers at D', the records pull request changed only records files (change detection's `records-only` is `true`), D' sits directly on D (the first parent of D' is D, with no other merge in between), and every merge-integrity run since the previous checkpoint was green. The last two conditions are recorded in their own columns and recomputed from git by the same test. Local runtime QA at D' goes in the table above, never here.

The first checkpoint, CP-20261006-1 (VL-CI-001), was dispatched on 2026-10-06 at the batch-end `develop` tip `d561e850` (the merge of #515). Hosted verification passed at that D (protected-gate Go), and its records pull request #517 merged as D' `b9c9d89e`, but the checkpoint was NOT accepted: the acceptance upgrade rehearsal at D' found migration 177 (`20261007140000`) failing on populated data (VL-DB-002). The forward-only fix #518 merged as `09005014`, and CP-20261006-2 was dispatched at that tip on the same day; it supersedes CP-20261006-1, whose row is kept. Its records pull request #519 merged as D' `789d4f59`, directly on D, and hosted verification decided Go at that D, but CP-20261006-2 was NOT accepted either: the local browser QA at D' found that a later draft revision could not be issued on the quotation screen (rows D5-2 issue screen, en and ar). #520 fixed it, and the local retest at `8864871c` found no failure. CP-20261007-1 was dispatched at 2026-10-06T23:03Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `290217da` (the merge of #523; the batch is #521, #520, #522 and #523) and supersedes CP-20261006-2, whose row is kept. Its records pull request #524 merged as D' `57160cba`, directly on D; the first attempt of its PR CI run failed one web test on an intermittent date-field defect (VL-WEB-001, fixed by #525), and the rerun of the failed jobs concluded success. No local runtime QA at that D' is recorded, so the row's local QA cell stays blank. CP-20261007-2 was dispatched at 2026-10-07T07:28Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `206c3ca6` (the merge of #526; the batch is #525 and #526). Its records pull request #527 merged as D' `ea7b5834`, directly on D; the local runtime QA at that D' (table above) found no failure and left two rows not run. CP-20261007-3 was dispatched at 2026-10-07T19:03Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `bd85f4b8` (the merge of #530; the batch is #529, #528 and #530). Its records pull request #531 merged as D' `3cf622c3`, directly on D; the local runtime QA at that D' (table above) found three print failures, regressions from #528 that #533 fixed and that are not accepted until retested, and left twelve rows not run, so its State stays pending. The CP-20261007-2 runtime QA evidence at `ea7b5834` (22 / 0 / 2; PQ-1-PAG not run, because no long quotation existed) was corrected after independent verification, and the corrections were rechecked on 2026-10-08 (outside repository, `orchestration/evidence/owner-directive-2026-10-07-datefield-d10-checkpoint/RESULT-MATRIX.md:54`); that row's local QA cell is not rewritten, because a register row only gains values in blank cells, and its State stays pending. CP-20261008-1 was dispatched at 2026-10-08T00:40Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `4202faa0` (the merge of #533; the batch is #532 and #533). Its records pull request #534 merged as D' `428d8eba`, directly on D; the local runtime QA at that D' (table above) found no product failure, recorded one evidence-quality failure (four dialog screenshots captured while the dialog was still fading in) and left five rows not run, so its State stays pending. CP-20261008-2 was dispatched at 2026-10-08T14:22Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `0c9122e6` (the merge of #537; the batch is #535, #536 and #537). A records pull request cannot name its own merge, so the CP-20261008-2 row's records pull request run, D', first parent of D', records-only and local QA cells stay blank until the next records pull request fills them from git and that run. Its records pull request #538 merged as D' `d5ce97b7`, directly on D; the CP-20261008-3 records pull request fills those cells from git and #538's run, the local runtime QA at that D' (table above) found no failure and left no row not run, and its State stays as written. CP-20261008-3 was dispatched at 2026-10-08T22:14Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `3dcad381` (the merge of #543; since CP-20261008-2's D: #538 (records), #541, #542, #539, #540 and #543). Its row's records pull request run, D', first parent of D', records-only and local QA cells stay blank until the next records pull request fills them from git and that run. Its records pull request #547 merged as D' `5f506e0f`, directly on D; the CP-20261009-1 records pull request fills those cells from git and #547's run, the local runtime QA at that D' (table above) found five failed checks and left four not run, and its State stays as written. CP-20261009-1 was dispatched at 2026-10-09T12:38Z at the batch-end `develop` tip `42de961f` (the merge of #559; since CP-20261008-3's D: #547 (records), #548, #546, #545, #544, #549, #550, #555, #551 and #559). Its row's records pull request run, D', first parent of D', records-only and local QA cells stay blank until the next records pull request fills them from git and that run.

**Note, 2026-10-08 (P1-32-PRE-OD-WP00).** A filled cell of this register is append-only, and that includes the State cell: no State above has been changed since it was written, and this pull request changes none. So a checkpoint's local runtime acceptance is read from its revision's row in "Checkpoints this matrix relies on" above, not from this table's State column, which records only whether full hosted verification is recorded. The CP-20261008-2 row's blank cells (records pull request and its STRICT run, D', first parent of D', records-only, local QA) are owed to the next checkpoint records pull request; this pull request is not that one and leaves them blank. CP-20261008-2's runtime QA at `d5ce97b7`, the merge of its records pull request #538, is the checkpoint table's row `d5ce97b7`. This note changes no rule of `tests/ci/verification-ledger.test.ts`.

### Verification ledger

Verification state only, under TDP-2026-10. Exactly five states exist: _Passed locally_, _Passed targeted hosted checks_, _Full hosted verification pending_, _Blocked by missing access or an unanswered business decision_, and _Passed full checkpoint verification_. A deferred check is never a pass. Counts are cited from `docs/phase-1/phase-1-27/evidence/local-run-ledger.json`, never restated here. Evidence outside the repository is cited as a path and revision labelled "outside repository". `tests/ci/verification-ledger.test.ts` enforces the states, the evidence each state needs, and that no other column claims a pass.

| VL-P132-005 | ADR-023 D10, print permissions: D10 asks that prints sit behind print permissions. Dedicated print permission codes are a permission transition tied to open question Q9 (`docs/product/owner-directive-2026-09-16/README.md`, question 9; CC-OD-50), so none is minted. Planner decision, not an Owner policy: each print is gated by the same read permissions as its page (quotation print: `quo.quotation.read`; credit-note print: `sal.credit.manage` and `sal.finance.view`), so nobody can print what they cannot read on screen **Note to VL-P132-015 and VL-WEB-002 (P1-32-PRE-OD-FRXR, 2026-10-09; no ledger row changed).** The
CP-20261008-3 runtime retest failed three classes, now fixed on branch
`feature/owner-directive-residual-fixes-r`, cut from `develop` at
`5f506e0fb4d575cc995ba174d1077fca1fb8ffed`: (FRX1-c) `iam.auth-session` and
`iam.working-context-read` answered 200 and ignored `userId`, `companyId` and `branchId` query
parameters; they now refuse any query parameter with the standard validation error (422
`ERR-VAL-001`) and no facts; (FRX2-nothing) the invoice screen said nothing when no approved work
remained, and now says so in words, en and ar, and a refused create keeps the payer typed into the
form; (FRX3) the printed invoice, counter sale and quotation no longer put the totals, or the
quotation's closing note, on a page without the content before them. State of the new cases:
_Passed locally_ on the working tree of that branch — `tests/backend/p1-24-iam-route-depth.test.ts`
on a disposable PostgreSQL 17 on 127.0.0.1:55458 replayed through all 187 migrations and the
declared seeds, `apps/web/tests/invoices.dom.test.tsx`, and
`apps/web/tests/e2e/print-layout.spec.ts` in real Chromium, en and ar; each new case was also run
against the code before the fix and failed there. The hosted result is recorded with its pull
request, not here.

## Owner directive of 2026-10-01: accounting invariants, approved product policy and per-organisation configuration

The 2026-10-01 directive asks for configurable commercial policy to be kept apart from accounting invariants, and for no financial policy to be invented. This section keeps three things apart: what the code enforces as an invariant, what ADR-023 approves as product policy, and what each organisation configures. It cites ADR-023 by line and does not restate its rules. Implementation state of each decision is ADR-023's mapping table (`:751-773` at develop `d5ce97b7`, re-anchored 2026-10-08; earlier `:703-721`); verification state is the delivery matrix above.

**The count, kept apart from the 97 capability rows:** the decision table in part 3 holds 16 rows (D5 and D15 share one).

### 1. Accounting invariants the code already enforces

These are code facts, not ADR-023 policy, and keeping them needs no questionnaire answer. No module reads a company or branch settings row (`docs/platform/architecture-assessment-2026-10-01.md` section 5), so no current setting reaches them; FC-19 proposes making that structural.

- **Issued documents are frozen.** `sal.guard_invoice_freeze` admits `issued -> credited` only (current definition `20260917093000_sal_counter_sales.sql:88-123`; trigger `20260724091000_sal_invoices.sql:133`; `billing/index.ts:54-56`). Stock from an issued counter sale returns only through a sales return (`20260917093000_sal_counter_sales.sql:34-39`).
- **Document amounts are snapshots.** Quotation lines carry a captured rate and amount checked by a trigger (`20260930100000_sal_minor_unit_rounding.sql:102-130`). Invoice lines are copied from the approved revision (`invoice-service.ts:825-840`).
- **Money is computed by the database in numeric**, and no client supplies an amount except a credit note's, which is bounded (`billing/index.ts:37-49`).
- **Financial events are append-only.** SELECT and INSERT grants only (`20260724093000_sal_financial_events.sql:123-124`). Amount and currency are bound to the source row (`:61-110`). One event per source and event type (`:53`).
- **The stock ledger is append-only.** SELECT and INSERT grants only (`20260723094000_inv_ledger.sql:97-98`). One movement per source and direction (`:80`).
- **Balances are derived, not stored.** The open receivable comes from `sal.invoice_open_receivable` (`20260724093000_sal_financial_events.sql:129-150`). Stock balances are coherence-guarded to movements and reservations (`20260723094000_inv_ledger.sql:129`).
- **Recovery is roll-forward only** for financial records (`20260724093000_sal_financial_events.sql:9-10`; `docs/phase-1/phase-1-11/phase-1-11-roll-forward-only-recovery-note.md`).
- **Audit is append-only**, written only through `iam.audit_append` (`apps/api/src/server/audit/audit.ts:88-92`).
- **Document numbers have no fallback.** An unprovisioned sequence is a configuration error (`billing/index.ts:50-53`).

### 2. Configurable commercial policy today

Each organisation sets these. None is an accounting invariant.

- Price lists, versions, rules, assignments, discount rules and pricing approval policies (`20260723092000_svc_pricing.sql:80-355`).
- Item selling prices by branch, company or everywhere (`20260917092000_inv_item_sale_prices.sql:54`; `capability-status.md:155`).
- Approval limits per type, currency, role or person within a company, including D13's credit-note limits (ADR-023 `:196-201`, `:392-393`).
- Who holds each permission code: the organisation's role grants.
- Tax classes and rates, designed as company configuration (`20260717105000_org_settings_tax_features.sql:193-194,244-245`). There is no write path yet (PPD-03): FC-20 is a design for the structure, held with the values until the questionnaire is answered.

### 3. D1 to D17: what can proceed, what waits on the questionnaire

ADR-023 line numbers are given in place of the rule text. The implementation-state column, the `(:759)` in row D3 and the mapping-table pointers in the overlaps list below cite the mapping table at develop `d5ce97b7` (re-anchored 2026-10-08, P1-32-PRE-OD-WP00); the other ADR-023 line numbers in this section are not re-anchored here. ADR-023 `:5-9` describes the decisions as approved product policy, not legal or accounting advice. Where ADR-023 says nothing, the cell reads none stated.

### Overlaps to keep in one place

- **Tax treatment.** D1 (inclusive rounding), D6 (snapshot), D10 (legal fields), the finance checkpoint's tax cases and CC-OD-48 (a missing class read as untaxed). One questionnaire answer unblocks all of them. The structural write path, FC-20, is held with them as a design until the questionnaire is answered; whether its structural part may go ahead earlier is an Owner question (`README.md`, open decision 10).
- **Receipt and refund instruments.** D2, D4 and D14. ADR-023 plans D4 and D14 together (`:760`, `:771`). D2's refund obligation should reuse the same approval pattern.
- **Quotation-to-invoice path.** D5, D15, D6 and D11, planned together (`:761-762`, `:767`).
- **As-of settlement.** D10 and D16 (`:766`, `:772`).
- **Costing.** D6 (never at cost, `:109`), D9 (valuation), inventory opening values and cost of goods sold. None may assume a method.
- **Permission transitions.** D13 (`:320-327`), D17, any D10 print code, and the tax permission (FC-20) each change who may do or see what on upgrade. All are tracked in one row, CC-OD-50. D17 as delivered by P1-32-PRE-OD-FD17 changes no grant; its transition arises only if the Owner narrows invoice records for a `sal.invoice.manage` holder without `sal.finance.view` (ADR-023 `:693-697`).
- **D16 against the directive's accounting scope.** D16 builds no new accounting subsystem (`:258`). The 2026-10-01 directive puts full accounting in scope, pending the questionnaire. D16's independent part stays inside the existing report. If the questionnaire calls for an accounting subsystem, a new ADR records it and supersedes that point. ADR-023 is not edited.

## Completion plan 2026-10-08 crosswalk

Imported 2026-10-08 (P1-32-PRE-OD-WP00) from the RootLco Completion and Release Plan, execution baseline 2026-10-08 (outside the repository). The plan's work packages (WP00 to WP18) and decision labels are navigation references: they are not phase numbers, ticket ids or states. **This section carries no status.** An item's live state is in the records it points to: this file's tables and ledger rows, the `README.md` questions, the `change-control.md` rows, ADR-023, ADR-024 and the phase records. Where the plan and the records differ, the difference is stated with its date, and either its resolution or the open question. Line numbers are at this pull request's head. Abbreviations: CS = this file; RM = `README.md`; CC = `change-control.md`; RC = `route-checklist.md` (all in this folder); A23 = `docs/adr/ADR-023-sales-and-finance-policy-decisions.md`; ADR-024 = `docs/adr/ADR-024-module-entitlements-and-commercial-packaging.md`; ADR-022 = `docs/adr/ADR-022-adopt-material-ui-and-mui-x-community.md`; OWR = `docs/product/owner-requirements-2026-09-06.md`; PKT = `docs/phase-1/phase-1-31/owner-decision-packet-2026-09-16.md`.

### (iii) The 200 original requirement ids

The plan's section 23: each id with its original scope and its destination package, and where the id already appears in a repository record (traceability files first; at most two pointers). The exact requirement text and acceptance wording live in the canonical Master documentation and in the external request workbook's Original Requirements sheet, both outside the repository, and are preserved by reference, not copied. No central catalogue of the ids exists in the repository; 80 of the 200 appear in at least one record, and 120 appear in none.

Id-format conflicts, recorded here as items to verify against the Master documentation and not resolved by this section:

- **NFR-PERF-01 against NFR-PERF-001.** `docs/phase-1/phase-1-24/evidence/task-traceability.md:84-92` records that the performance requirement is `NFR-PERF-01`, and that `NFR-PERF-001` appears only in that correction; `docs/phase-1/phase-1-32/canonical-plan.md:127` and the plan cite `NFR-PERF-001`.
- **NFR-SEC-001, NFR-DAT-001, NFR-MNT-001 and NFR-OBS-001 have no definition row** in the repository (the same file, `:84-92`); the two-digit `NFR-SEC-01` to `NFR-SEC-03` of the P1-12 database matrix are different requirements.
- **FR-INT-001 and FR-INT-002** are traced as delivered in P1-13 (`docs/phase-1/phase-1-13/phase-1-13-traceability.md:22-23`, `docs/phase-1/phase-1-13/phase-1-13-plan.md:87`), while the plan sends them to WP18.
- The plan gives every family a uniform five-id list; the repository's per-phase records use the ids each phase traced, so an id with no pointer below is not shown to be undelivered.
